- [UK campus students | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/international-students/uk-campus-students)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: You may be aware of the recent UK Government announcement on the Immigration White Paper regarding international students and other immigration routes which will involve changes to the UK’s visa regulations. Please be aware that these changes will not take immediate effect and may take months to enact into law and some new policies might not even be in place until next year. Heriot-Watt welcomes the confirmation that the Graduate Post-Study Work route will remain open and competitive although with a reduced duration. That means that international students can remain in the UK and work for up to 18 months after completing their studies, gaining experience, and helping to address critical skills shortages. Dependants on all routes including the student route will eventually be required to pass a basic English language assessment if they are applying for a Dependant visa, we do not know when this will come into place. The route to short term settlement will increase from 5 years to 10 years even if someone is on the skilled migrant visa. Please be assured that international students continue to be warmly received at Heriot-Watt. Your contribution is hugely valued and your decision to study here is extremely important to us. We will support you at every stage of your student journey. Our University is proud of its status worldwide as a culturally diverse, warm and welcoming institution. Our global footprint includes campuses in Dubai, Malaysia, Edinburgh, the Scottish Borders and Orkney. Our doors remain firmly open to international students. If you have any questions about studying with us, please get in touch with us at studywithus@hw.ac.uk . We look forward to welcoming you soon.

  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: We're here to support your young person and their future. Learn more about what joining Heriot-Watt involves and how you can support your young person throughout the university experience. Once your young person has decided on which programmes and universities they'd like to apply to, they will make their application through UCAS (the University and Colleges Admissions Service). They will make just one application that contains five choices and one personal statement. This personal statement must be relevant to all five choices. Find out more on our application page . - [Swimming Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/swimming)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: The Heriot-Watt Swimming Club is a GOLD Watt Mark level club and was awarded Club of the Year in 2022. As our club continues to grow, we are looking forward to giving the opportunity for all levels of swimming to improve. This is done through our head coach and former Commonwealth Games swimmer, Cameron Brodie. Along with our 5 student coaches and dryland fitness training once a week, we aim to give comprehensive training and support. With regular swimming sessions and exclusive social events for members, we aim to provide all our swimmers with a memorable sporting and social experience whilst at University. We welcome swimmers of all levels, whether you are looking to improve your skills, maintain general fitness, or compete at a higher level. Our sessions cater to various goals, offering tailored coaches to meet the needs of every swimmer. Join us to be part of our vibrant and inclusive community! Yes, you need to be able to swim at least 25 metres, but the club now offers a ‘Learn to Swim’ course which is designed to help beginners build their confidence and improve their swimming skills. This course is a great opportunity for those who are new to swimming to receive instruction and support from our experienced teacher Lauren Sandison.
- [Boat Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/boat)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Rowing is a fantastic sport. Whether it’s on your own or working as part of a crew to get the boat moving fast on rivers and Olympic grade courses. With crew sizes up to 8 there’s and the option for endurance or sprints, there is somewhere for you to fit in. Even for those who feel for whatever reason they don’t fit into typical sports we offer the opportunity to cox. A cox is integral to a good crew and is responsible for issuing commands, keeping the crew in time and steering the boat down the course. We have three squads that enter competitions throughout the academic year: Senior Men, Senior Women and Beginner. Competitions include Inverness 4’s and Small Boats Head in November, and other local races. We also head to BUCS Head in Newcastle and the Head of River Race on the Thames through London. The club also attends a range of regattas including BUCS Regatta in Nottingham and the Scottish Rowing Championships.
- [Ultimate Frisbee Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/ultimatefrisbee)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: If you are looking to keep fit, start a new sport at university, or find a club with great socials and an even better community, you’re in the right place. Ultimate Frisbee is a sport that you can learn to play in an afternoon, and love for the rest of your life. Whether you’re a high-performance athlete looking for some extra cardio and have a blast doing it, or new to sports and want a fun way to get active and involved, the Ultimate Frisbee Club has something for you. This is a team sport that can be played with mixed, men’s or women’s teams. The goal is to score as many points as possible by catching the disc in the end-zone (like in American Football). The only catch (apart from the ones you’ll be doing of course) is that you cannot move while in possession of the disc.

- [Browzer Engage](https://halls.wattliving.co.uk/accessibility)
  Site: Watt Living
  Summary: Browzer from CampusLife is an award-winning communications tool designed to help get your messages across. From confirming their place, right the way up to graduation day, our innovative and easy-to- use Browzer platform helps students make the most of their time at university. The platform is fully supported by our team — we’re able to manage day-to-day running of the site, create content, analyse data, and build relationships with key stakeholders to ensure success. Browzer is WCAG 2.1 compliant. Due to the nature of white-labels (custom university designs) and dynamic user content sometimes the WCAG 2.1 compliance is not fully met and we explain this in our custom accessibility statement for your university.

## Student Support (554)

- [Chaplaincy | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/uk/students/health-wellbeing/edinburgh/faith/chaplaincy.htm)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: The Chaplaincy at Heriot-Watt University, Edinburgh campus.
- [Dentist | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/health-and-wellbeing/edinburgh-campus/dentist)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: The Riccarton Dental Practice is located in the Medical Centre. It offers a full range of dental treatments under the NHS or private treatments if requested. The majority of patients will have to pay for their dental care. Only patients in possession of an HC-2 certificate, or who are on income support or other benefits will be exempt from charges. Please call the practice or visit to schedule an appointment.
- [Doctor | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/health-and-wellbeing/edinburgh-campus/doctor)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: At our Heriot-Watt Edinburgh Campus, we have a purpose-built health centre housing the University Health Service and Dental Practice, which provides a full range of UK National Health Service (NHS) medical and dental services.
- [Environment and sustainability | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/health-and-wellbeing/edinburgh-campus/environment-and-sustainability)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Our Campus Services is responsible for the development and implementation of our environmental, energy and sustainability policies. We have a carbon management plan and various initiatives in place to reduce our energy consumption and to promote recycling, and each year we submit a climate change report to the Sustainable Scotland Network (SSN). We hope that while you are studying with us, you can play your part in helping us to minimise waste, reduce our energy use and recycle as much as possible.

  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Heriot-Watt University respects religious and cultural diversity and aims to support individuals in their religious and cultural observance. We aim to be a place where students and staff of different cultures, backgrounds and beliefs can meet and mix together in a friendly, welcoming environment. On these pages you will find information on:

  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: We are committed to ensuring the health and safety of all our students. While the University will do its upmost to ensure that the campus, and the accommodation within it, is safe and secure, there is also a need for you to take responsibility for staying safe. On these pages you can:
- [Orkney Campus | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/health-and-wellbeing/orkney-campus)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Contact the Orkney Campus Disability Service for advice, guidance and the support that enables you to fulfil your potential as a Heriot-Watt student.
- [Scottish Borders Campus | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/students/health-and-wellbeing/scottish-borders-campus)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: In line with the Scottish Government’s commitment to provide access to free sanitary products, Heriot Watt University makes available a wide range of products to students and staff. We are committed to supporting equality, dignity and rights for all those who menstruate and ensure that there is no impact on an individual’s ability to fully participate in education at all levels. Consultation and feedback from the student union and service users has been used to determine what provision of free period products was best suited to the needs of our community. The results were used to inform both the range of period products to be made available and how products would be distributed.

  Summary: Heriot-Watt University is committed to some Key Principles which form our pledge to the Armed Forces Covenant – and on these pages/this site you will see further information about how this works in practice: Set out below are some specific policies directly relating to our commitment in respect of training, volunteering and general wellbeing support: All of our Staff Policies are also available.

  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Our Residence Life team is here to ensure the welfare and wellbeing of all resident students living on the Heriot-Watt campus.
- [SafeGuarding Services | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/uk/services/safeguarding.htm)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Heriot-Watt SafeGuarding Services provide a vital role in safeguarding the University’s population, assets and reputation. We are pivotal in delivering a strong care and support service to the students and staff who make up the Heriot-Watt community. SafeGuarding Services operate 24 hours a day, 365 days a year and our SafeGuarding Officers are the first responders to any incidents occurring on campus. All of our SafeGuarding Officers are first aid and mental health first aid trained and will respond to all SafeZone activations on campus.
- [The James Watt Building | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/dubai/campus/the-dubai-campus/james-watt-building)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Located just a minute from the main campus, the James Watt Building offers 48,000 sq. ft. of modern social and learning spaces, purpose-built to elevate your student experience. Opened in 2025 to celebrate Heriot-Watt’s 20th anniversary in Dubai, the building was shaped by student feedback and designed to meet the needs of our growing community. The James Watt building has been designed with student learning and wellbeing at its heart, featuring a wide range of modern facilities and collaborative spaces. Along with classrooms and digital learning environments, students enjoy access to the student hub and learning commons spaces that foster both academic and social engagement.

- [Badminton Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/badminton)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: The Heriot-Watt Badminton Club is your go-to place for all things badminton. Whether you’re here to learn the basics, stay active, or push your game to the next level, we offer a friendly and inclusive environment where you can enjoy badminton, meet new people, and be part of a supportive, fun community. Badminton is a fast-paced racquet sport played indoors on a court divided by a net. Whether in singles (1v1) or doubles (2v2), players score points by hitting a shuttlecock over the net and into the opponent’s side of the court. It’s a game of speed, agility, reflexes, and strategy , making it perfect for everyone — from complete beginners to seasoned athletes. Whether you’re here for fun, improvement, or serious competition, Heriot-Watt Badminton Club has something for everyone. Here’s how we break it down:
- [Volunteer Awards 20/21 – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/celebrate/volunteer-awards-20-21)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Sport Union Experience: 17/18 Basketball Treasurer, 18/19 & 19/20 Basketball President, 18/19 SU Event Ambassador, 20/21 SU Lead Health & Wellbeing Ambassador, 20/21 Basketball Club Community Officer. The S ports Union has been a staple experience throughout my 5 years at Heriot-Watt University. After my first year, I was keen to get involved with my beloved basketball club as part of the committee and enhance my CV. It soon became a real passion of mine to give back to the club and Sports Union as I was quick to realise sport is so much more than a physical activity. It’s a lifestyle and escape from the everyday University stress. The Watt family is a home away from home and being a part of offering an exceptional experience to other students has undoubtedly left me with my most memorable moments throughout University.” Sport Union Experience: 16/17 & 17/18 Boat Club Equipment Manager, 20/21 Boat Club Secretary.

## Accommodation and Living (88)

- [Accommodation | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/campuses/edinburgh/accommodation)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: We have 2000 rooms of student accommodation in self-catering halls of residences on campus at Heriot-Watt University's Edinburgh Campus.
- [Exchange | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/campuses/edinburgh/accommodation/exchange)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: There will be a limited number of rooms on campus for single semester. Please note that the number of rooms is finite and there is no guarantee that an application will be successful. Exchange and visiting students studying at the Edinburgh campus can apply for accommodation once they hold a Conditional Firm or Unconditional Firm offer to study. Offers of accommodation will only be made once you hold an Unconditional Firm offer to study at Heriot-Watt University. If you are coming for a full year (40 weeks) you are eligible to apply for contemporary ensuite room in a flat or traditional ensuite .
- [Meet our students | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/malaysia/study/meet-our-students)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Find out what our current students and recent graduates say about life here in the Malaysia campus, and the advice that they would offer students considering Heriot-Watt University Malaysia.

  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Living on campus is a great way to meet others and we have a range of safe and comfortable accommodation within minutes of teaching buildings and the Student Union.

- [Future years accommodation | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/campuses/edinburgh/accommodation/future-years-accommodation)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: We have a small numbers of rooms available for returning students for 2026/27. Applications open on Monday 2 February 2026 where students are required to make a new application through the accommodation portal. These rooms are subject to availability and are not covered by the accommodation guarantee.

- [American Football Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/americanfootball)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: American football is a sport based on athleticism, skill and strategy. The closest sport is rugby league with a limited set of plays to work your team down the field and score. Take back every pre-conception of American football in the U.K. and come down to give it a go. It is suitable for everybody and the perfect team is like a zoo. Come join the best group of people on campus. This is the cheapest and easiest it’ll ever be to try a new sport so come and give it a go now and you will never look back We have a team that competes in Bucs Div 1 Scotland having gone undefeated to gain promotion last year. If you’re looking for a casual version of the sport we have flag football on a Wednesday. Our club trains on Tuesday and Friday evenings on the outdoor and indoor astro pitches with non contact training taking place on a Wednesday afternoon. Time and details are posted on the Instagram, otherwise feel free to contact the team Instagram HWUAFC or club President DylanJones2003
- [Athletics Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/athletics)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Before the revival of the Summer Olympic games in 1896, the first ever recorded Olympics took place in 776BC, athletics was one of the only events alongside boxing and wrestling to not be discontinued and continue as a sport through to the modern Olympics. Running and sprinting are the oldest sports in history with depictions found in caves from around 15,300 years ago. Athletics has always been a popular sport and still is today and so we welcome you to our club, whether you are already a club runner or just looking to improve your fitness we have something here for you. From sprinting to long distance running and throwing and jumping you’ll find a place at Heriot-Watt Athletics club. Our club caters for a wide range of competitions including cross country, track and field, road and hill running, and orienteering. Along with our large range of competitions and training we have to offer we also organise a range of different social events throughout the year which gives members the chance to get to know everyone in the club.
- [Ice Skating Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/clubs/ice-skating-club)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: The Heriot-Watt University Ice Skating Club is the newest fully fledged Sports Union club as of 2024! We welcomed our first members in September 2023 and our vibrant community is always welcoming new members. Our committee is currently home to fans of Ice Hockey, Figure and Recreational Ice Skating in equal amounts – no matter what form of Ice Skating you’re interested in, or what skill level you are at, you are definitely welcome alongside us. We look forward to seeing you at our sessions on the ice and/or for some fun times at our socials to make some unforgettable memories throughout your university experience! Our committee is also a very welcoming and friendly bunch, and we are more than happy to chat and provide more information as requested. For the latest information, check out our Instagram page and feel free to reach out if you have any questions!
- [Lifting Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/lifting)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Heriot Watt Lifting Club is one of the newest HW sports union clubs brought to Oriam. Whether you’re just getting into fitness or you’ve had a lot of experience training, the HW Lifting Club has something for you. The club is comprised of two training disciplines. These are Olympic Weightlifting and Powerlifting. Olympic weightlifting is a sport whereby an athlete lifts a loaded barbell from the floor to an overhead position. The two competing lifts for the sport are the snatch and the clean and jerk. This sport is very technical and has a lot of room for growth. Last year we had two members of the club go on to compete at ‘Scottish Universities’, one of whom joined the club not having tried the sport before.
- [Table Tennis Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/tabletennis)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Welcome to the page for Table Tennis! Our sport is a mixture of fast paced action and a relaxed, fun time with friends! Table Tennis is easily accessible to all ages and skill levels. We play on a rectangular table with a small net in the middle, and we use a small plastic ball and hit it over the table with bats. It can be played just for fun, or competitively so we hope you come along to try it out! We currently have 2 teams, one in division 2 and one in division 3 of the Edinburgh Lothian League. We are in the process of getting another team to put into division 4 as well to give a better opportunity for beginners to get into the sport and competition. We also compete in BUCS against other university teams. In BUCS we compete mainly within Scotland, but also have the possibility of qualifying to the national UK tournament! If you are not up for competing we run a recreational session and a club session each week to relax and have a good time! Our club is for all things Table Tennis, we have recreational sessions as well as team trainings. We play to relax as well as competing in Edinburgh, Scotland, and the UK. We have multiple training sessions a week, located in the Table Tennis Studio, Squash Courts, and Dance Studio 1.

- [Heritage Trail | Heriot-Watt University](https://www.hw.ac.uk/campuses/edinburgh/campus-trails/heritage-trail)
  Site: Heriot-Watt University
  Summary: Welcome to the Heriot-Watt University Heritage Trail The Heritage Trail is designed to guide you through some of the history and art that is embroidered into the very fabric of the campus. We are extremely fortunate to have a wonderful blend of historical structures and features through to modern sculptures and buildings woven into such a beautiful landscape. The Heritage trail map will show you the route. There are 25 points of interest on this trail but so much more to appreciate as you meander through this specially designed trail. We hope you enjoy the experience!

- [Aikido Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/aikido)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Aikido is defensive martial art. The style of Aikido practised at this club is Shodokan aikido. Shodokan Aikido combines traditional Aikido techniques with the principles of competitive sport, making it somewhat unique among Aikido styles. Here are some key features and aspects of Shodokan Aikido: Sportive Element: Our style incorporates a form of sparring called “Randori,” where participants engage in controlled, live training scenarios. Randori allows students to apply Aikido techniques against a fully resisting opponent, promoting practical application and testing of their skills.
- [Hockey Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/hockey)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Hockey is a fun and inclusive sport whether or not you have played before it is a simple and enjoyable sport to pick up. Teams of 11, players play with a stick and pass the ball around looking to break into the shooting circle and score into a goal! The Heriot Watt hockey club has been one of the largest sports club the university has seen for the past few years. Men’s 1 – BUCS Scottish Division 1A Men’s 2 – BUCS Scottish division 3 Ladies 1 – BUCS Scottish Division 2 Ladies 2 – BUCS Scottish Division 4 Training is once a week at Peffermill playing fields on a Monday evening from 8:30-10pm. Transport will be available for those travelling from Campus. All are truly welcome!
- [Netball Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/netball)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Never heard of netball? Here’s a bit about us… Netball is a ball sport that lasts for 4, 15minute quarters which involves two teams, consisting of 7 players each, trying to score the most points by shooting into a net. Each player has a unique area of the court they are allowed in and must work together with teammates in order to get the ball up the court to goal. There’s some extra challenges like you cannot move with the ball plus netball is classed as a low contact sport, have a wee look here to see our sport in action: https://youtu.be/bumBd6Q0ucA The club is run by our hardworking all student committee, who go above and beyond to welcome new and returning members to take part in netball. Whether you want to try netball for the first time, haven’t played since school or are already competitive we are the perfect club for you! We have regular socials where we get up to all kinds of fun and choas; from fancy dress, to balls, ceilidhs, sober nights in, comfy movie nights, pub nights and dinners, we have it all! We finish every academic year with a big club tour, which is organised by our tour secretary with previous destinations like Barcelona, Algrave and Budapest. We have 4 squads, with trials taking place in Semester 1 during weeks 0-2. Our squad matches are played on a Saturday as part of the BUCS league as well as the Conference Cup matches throughout the season and a one-off SSS (Scottish Student Sport) Tournament. The CENA team also plays games on a Sunday evenings. For non-competitive members we also have opportunities throughout the year to get involved with like fun recreational tournaments.
- [Rugby Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/rugby)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Rugby is an electrifying contact team sport that blends the intensity of American football with the continuous flow of football. Played on a rectangular pitch, the goal is to carry the rugby ball across the opposing team’s goal line to score points, or kick the ball through the opposing teams goal posts. Unlike many sports, players can only pass the ball backward or sideways, demanding teamwork and strategy to advance. Once a player is tackled, the game doesn’t stop; instead, it continues with both teams competing for possession of the ball in a dynamic battle known as the ruck or maul. Rugby’s physicality and respect for opponents make it a thrilling and honourable game. Whether you’re a player or a spectator, rugby offers an exhilarating experience that celebrates teamwork, skill, and pure determination. So, if you’re looking for an action-packed sport with a touch of elegance and camaraderie, rugby might just be the perfect fit! HWURFC is a club that consists of over 100 members, including players and social members. With rugby training and Strength and Conditioning sessions twice a week as well as matches and social events every Wednesday, everyone is welcome no matter your rugby experience. Men’s Head Coach – Dave Hampton
- [Snowsports Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/snowsports)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: At university, skiing and snowboarding offer exhilarating winter sports experiences that are sure to captivate both beginners and seasoned enthusiasts. Picture gliding down snow-covered slopes, carving graceful turns, and feeling the rush of adrenaline as you explore snowy landscapes. Whether you prefer the smooth elegance of skiing or the thrilling manoeuvres of snowboarding, these activities provide a fantastic way to stay active, connect with nature, and create unforgettable memories with friends. So, if you haven’t tried skiing or snowboarding yet, it’s an adventure waiting for you on the snowy slopes. We offer lessons for beginners to competitions for the more advanced. We also run regular social events throughout the year, and membership is open to anyone through the Oriam app. The club is run by a committee of students who are all mad about skiing, snowboarding and everything that goes with it. We cater for everyone from total beginners to those who are just in it for the après, and even those who want to compete at a national level in skiing and snowboarding. Delve into our top-notch training sessions held at the renowned Hillend dry slopes every week. Here, you’ll have the incredible opportunity to opt for lessons tailored to your needs or even embrace the thrill of team spirit by joining our prestigious competitive team. Embrace the magic of snowsports with us, where unforgettable adventures and remarkable camaraderie await your university experience!
- [Taekwondo Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/taekwondo)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Taekwondo literally means “The Art Of Hand And Foot” but it is much more than that. To put it simply Taekwondo is a version of unarmed combat design for the purpose of self-defence. It is the scientific use of the body in the method of self-defence, a body that has gained the ultimate use of its facilities through intensive physical and mental training. It is a martial art that has no equal in either power or technique. As Taekwondo is a complete martial art, the discipline of the art must go hand in hand with learning the physical advantages of doing such a powerful art. The first lesson that the students learn are the five tennents of Taekwondo, which are: Courtesy, Integrity, Perseverance, Self Control and Indomitable Spirt. Want to increase your fitness, improve your confidence, or just pick up some self-defence skills? Then Taekwondo could be the sport for you.
- [Cheerleading Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/cheerleading)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Cheerleading originally involved participants chanting phrases in order to motivate and encourage their team at sports matches and entertain the crowd watching the sport. But cheerleading has evolved and is no longer just girls with pom-poms at the sidelines of high school football matches, it has changed massively over the last couple of decades and now involves gravity-defying stunts! While cheerleading in high schools still exists, the sport is now more focused on all-star teams competing against one another. Cheerleading routines are made up of four elements: stunts, jumps, tumble and dance. Teams are judged on technique, execution, synchronisation, and overall performance. The International Olympic Committee officially recognised cheerleading as a sport in 2021 and now we’ll hopefully see cheerleading in the 2028 Olympics in Los Angeles! Heriot-Watt Cheerleading Club received club status within the Sports Union in 2014. Our club is focussed on All-Star cheer, welcoming all levels of ability and experience; it doesn’t matter if you have no experience whatsoever in cheerleading, gymnastics or dance, you’re welcome to join us!
- [Kayak Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/kayak)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Our club is a quite small amongst some of the other SU giants, but we make up for that by being a friendly and welcoming environment. We cater to a range of skill sets – whether you’ve spent years on the water or never set foot on a boat, we’ve got you covered. Joining our club is an amazing way to see some of the natural scenery of Scotland, from the borders to Glencoe, we do trips to various rivers and lochs every other Sunday, plenty of opportunity for an adventure – not to mention the occasional weekend trip. There are also plenty of socials and lots of super people to meet! Every year we participate in a kayak polo tournament at Pinkston involving a lot of other Scottish universities, we also compete in white water sprint events though out the year.
- [Equestrian Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/equestrian)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: HWU Equestrian Club is dedicated to providing opportunities for all levels of riders, from complete beginners who may have never even sat on a horse before to more advanced riders looking to compete. We offer weekly riding lessons with friendly, qualified instructors for beginners and intermediate riders who are not looking to compete. These lessons will consist of learning how to ride and improving your skills as well as learning how to tack up and look after the horses in the stable. For those more competitive and advanced riders we have one team who represent the University and compete in the BUCS leagues. These competitions involve a novice dressage test and a short round of show jumps. Each team has three competitions in the BUCS league every year, a team consists of only four riders and reserves which are picked during team trials during October. A typical competition consists of a dressage test with a horse that you pick from random, you get a short warm up to get to know your horse before starting your test, a member from each other team will ride the same horse as you and you will be scored in comparison to each other.
- [Volleyball Club – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/volleyball)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
  Summary: Volleyball is a game played by two teams, usually of six players on a side, in which the players use their hands to bat a ball back and forth over a high net, trying to make the ball touch the court within the opponents’ playing area before it can be returned. As a club we welcome everyone of any ability to join in our sessions as we look to work on your skills throughout the year no matter how long you have played for. We offer competitive opportunities for those looking to join a team, we offer coaching for 3 teams (2 men’s and 1 women’s), and we offer plenty of completive opportunities for recreational members by holding in-house tournaments and enter teams into community 4v4 tournaments. We have 4 teams that compete in BUCS:
- [Volunteer Awards 21/22 – Heriot-Watt University Sports Union](https://sportsunion.site.hw.ac.uk/celebrate/awards-21-22)
  Site: Heriot-Watt Sports Union
